                                      All rights reserved
FOREWORD

Education is one of the main pillars upon which Rwanda's socio-economic
development hinges. For nearly the past three decades, Rwanda has registered
progressive improvement in education in terms of access, quality and equity. In
a bid to make education more relevant to national aspirations of becoming a
knowledge-based economy, a competence-based curriculum was introduced.
In view of this, national examinations are administered at the end of each learning
cycle (Primary 6, senior 3, senior 6 for general basic education, level 5 of Technical
and Vocational Education and Training (TVET) and end of year 3 for TTCs) to
assess the attainment of intended curriculum competences to inform decision
making and education system management.

As such, National Examination and School Inspection Authority (NESA) was
established with a prime mission of ensuring quality of education in basic
education and Technical and Vocational Education and Training (TVET) from level
one (1) to five (5) and to regulate the comprehensive assessments of students
and national examinations.

Accordingly, NESA conducted an analysis of national examination results to
establish the trend of learners' performance and pass rate to inform improvement
pathways in teaching, learning and assessment. The analysis covered all the
subjects examined in 2019, 2021, and 2022 school years.

On this note, NESA presents a report of the analysis of the national examination
results for each subject or trade at each level. The report highlights the general
pass rate and performance, pass rate disaggregated by gender, school location
(rural and urban), school ownership (public, private and government aided) and
school status (boarding/day school) for each subject.

It is hoped that this report provides a general picture of learners' achievement at
the end of the respective learning cycles, and informs education stakeholders of
areas for intervention.

We express our sincere gratitude to the World Bank for the financial support and
everyone who contributed to the successful compilation of this report.

Sincerely,

Dr. BAHATI Bernard

Director General
National Examination and School Inspection Authority (NESA)

xxviii  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

Analysis of student national examinations helps to determine the extent to which
the curriculum learning outcomes have been achieved and to identify areas
where they perform well and those that need improvement. This report presents
results of the analysis of national examinations for end of primary six, end of
Ordinary level, end of advanced level, end of level 5 of Technical and Vocational
Education and Training (TVET), and year three of teacher training colleges for three
consecutive school years (2019, 2021 and 2022). The objectives of the analysis
were twofold: 1) to compare the pass rate of students in the old and the new
grading system, and 2) to identify subjects that require intervention in terms of
teaching, learning & assessment to improve student's performance in the future.

Student performance in 2022 was analyzed based on the revised grading system
in comparison with the performance in previous years, that is, 2019 and 2021.
The analysis highlights the achievement level of learners, thus identifying their
strengths, weaknesses, and gaps in the teaching and learning process in schools
in Rwanda. The initiative of analyzing national examinations for primary six, lower
secondary level, upper secondary level, Teacher Training Colleges (TTCs) and
Technical and Vocational Education and Training (TVET) has been inspired by
other countries in the region such as Kenya, Uganda, Ghana and South Africa
where analysis of national examinations is conducted and elicits valuable
information for informed decision making by policy makers as well as informing
education supervisors at various levels.

Accordingly, the analysis began with collecting, organizing and synthesizing
results of students in all national examinations for 2019, 2021, and 2022 as well
as views of teachers. The results were retrieved from National Examination
and School Inspection Authority (NESA) examination data base, national
examination guidelines, chief markers' reports produced during marking of
national examinations, and teachers' views during focus group discussions
regarding national examination performance. Results of students were entered
into SPSS version 20, Stata14, Access, and excel for easy tabulation, analysis
and interpretation. Students' results were processed, categorized, coded, and
analyzed. Descriptive statistics (measures of central tendency and variability)
were used to analyse and interpret the findings. Charts, frequency tables, and cross
tabulations were generated. Data on student performance was categorized and
presented according school ownership (private, government aided and public
schools); gender; school location (districts and provinces, rural and urban); and
school status (boarding and day schools). The subjects for which the national
exam results were analysed at every level include: Primary level (5), O-level (9),
A-level (14 subject combinations), TTCs (18 subject combinations) and TVET 34
trades (216 subjects).

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  xxix
Key findings

Overall, results from the analysis of national examinations for each learning cycle
show that the pass rate of students in all the examinable subjects in 2022 (when
the revised grading system began) was relatively higher than in the previous two
years (2019 and 2021) in the old grading system. This suggests that the revised
grading system did not negatively impact on the pass rate of students.

The pass rate of students in national exams in the three years (2019, 2021 and 2022)
varied according to the learning cycle/level, subject, gender, school ownership
and type of school (boarding and day), among others. However, it should be
noted that for A-level, TTC, and TVET, there was no consistency in the pass rate
between male and female students over the three years. While pass rates among
female students were generally higher than male in some subject combinations
and trades, they were lower in others.

 At primary school level, the pass rate in private schools was relatively higher
than in public and Government aided school. However, in 2022, in which the
revised grading system began, the pass rate was relatively higher than in the two
previous year (2019 & 2021). Specifically, in 2019 and 2021 the pass rate stood at
81.6% and 81% respectively while in 2022, it increased to 90.69%. Disaggregated
by gender, the pass rate of girls was relatively higher than that of boys. Overall,
Kinyarwanda had the highest pass rate across all three years and Mathematics
had the lowest. The pass rate in rural schools was relatively lower than the pass
rate in urban schools in 2021 and 2022. On average, the pass in 2021 was 75.12% in
rural schools and 86.90% in urban while in 2022, it was 82.08% in rural schools and
89.48% in urban.

With respect to O-level, students' pass rate in all the subjects examined was
relatively consistent over the three school years. It stood at 86.6% in 2019, 86.3%
in 2021 and 85.7% in 2022.When disaggregated by gender, the trend of pass rate
was similar, but the pass rate among girls was relatively higher than boys. The
average pass rate for all the examined subjects at O-level significantly increased
over the three years. It rose from 56.8% in 2019 to 67.1% in 2021 and to 93.3% in 2022.
It is worth noting that while there was a substantial increase in the pass rate in all
the examinable subjects across the three years, Mathematics subject remained
having the lowest pass rate. The highest pass rate in each of the three years was
observed in Kinyarwanda. In contrast to primary school leaving exams in which
the pass rate among girls was predominantly higher than boys across the three
years, in O-level exams, the pass rate among boys in all exams was relatively
higher than girls in the three years.

For A-level, the general pass rate of candidates who sat for national examinations
varied. In 2019, it stood at 89.5% and reduced to 85.3% in 2021. However, in 2022,
it sharply rose to 94.6%. Among the core combination subjects for humanities,
the highest average pass rate was in Kiswahili (87.8%) and the lowest in French

xxx  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
(74.56%) across the three school years. Similarly, for science core combination
subjects, Biology had the highest average pass rate (91.58%) while Mathematics
had the least average pass rate (66.76%). General Studies and Communication
Skills, which is a non-core subject had the overall highest pass rate in the three
years (2019, 2021 & 2022). With respect to gender, the pass rate of male students
was relatively higher than that of female students in all the A-level examined
subject combinations. In addition, for A-level, type of school (boarding and day)
was considered and it was found that, in general, the pass rate in boarding schools
was relatively higher than day.

Regarding TTCs, the pass rate in year three TTC national examinations was
relatively consistent over the three school years considered (2019, 2021 and
2022), with no significant difference across the years. In 2019, the proportion of
students that passed was 98.2% while in 2021 and 2022 it stood at 99.7% and 99.9%
respectively. Nonetheless, there were variations in pass rate in the examined TTC
subjects over the three years. The subject with lowest average pass rate was
French (37.5%) while Foundations of Education, Mathematics, Entrepreneurship,
Teaching practices, English, and Kinyarwanda had the highest average pass rate
(over 95%). It is worth noting that the pass rate in some subjects was 100%. This
was evident in 2022 in subjects such as English, Kinyarwanda, Integrated Sciences
and Entrepreneurship.

The pass rate in other TTC subjects in 2022, when the revised grading system
started was generally outstanding ranging from 94% in Chemistry to 100% in
Economics, Geography, History, and Creative Performance. In fact, the average
pass rate was 98.80%. There was no significant difference in pass rate between
female and male students in other TTC subjects. For instance, in subjects such
as Creative Performance (Music & Fine Arts), Economics, Geography, History,
Religious Education, Social Studies, and Teaching Methods, the pass rate of female
and male students was either the same or very close to each other.

With respect to TVET, there were 34 trades in all the three years (2019, 2021 and
2022), and each trade consisted of 6-10 subjects in which students were examined
at national level. Overall, the pass rate in TVET trades national examinations
across the three years was very good. In 2019, the general pass rate was about
91% and in 2021 and 2022, it increased at nearly the same rate, that is, 97% and 98
% respectively. Another trade of Software programming and Embedded Systems
was introduced in 2022, with 17 subjects and the pass rate in 16 of them was 100%,
while in the other trade it stood at about 88%. The trades with the highest average
pass rates across the three years were: Graphics Art trade (99%), Ceramics and
Sculpture trade (99%), Interior design trade (98%), and Welding (98%). The lowest
pass rates were observed in Food and Beverage Services trade (70%), Customs
and taxations operations trade (76%) and accounting trade (76.7%). In addition
to the trades, the results of 6 cross cutting subjects were also analysed and the
highest pass rate was in Kinyarwanda (97%) while the lowest in Kiswahili (24%).

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  xxxi
In a nutshell, there was a progressive improvement in performance in national
examinations for primary, O-level, A-level, Teacher training colleges as well as
TVET trades over the three years. In fact, in 2022, the general pass rates of students
in exams at all levels were significantly higher compared to 2019 and 2021. The
implication is that the new grading system had no impact on student failing or
passing. Despite the progressive improvement in general performance at different
levels, as earlier mentioned, in some subjects and trades, the performance was
poor and there were variations, especially pertaining to location (rural-urban),
ownership of school (public, district, private and government aid) and gender.
Based on the findings of the analysis, the following recommendations are made:

      NESA should have a long-term plan for adopting and implementing this
          practice every year. Also, NESA should plan so that in future, end term III
          exam results are also analysed and comparison made with national exam
          results to provide a clear picture of student performance. It can also help
          teachers to evaluate the effectiveness of their teaching strategies.

      NESA, in collaboration with partners should conduct a comprehensive study
          to identify the factors that have contribute to disparities in performance by
          location, gender, district and school ownership. The findings of the study
          could serve as the basis for development of evidence-based strategies
          that can enhance the quality of teaching and learning thereby addressing
          disparities between provinces, rural and urban schools. The study can also
          unveil best practices in high-performing districts, and schools that can be
          adopted and implemented by those that perform poorly.

xxxii  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
SECTION ONE: INTRODUCTION

1.1. Background and Rationale for Analysis of National
     Examinations

Improving student learning outcomes is one of the key priorities for the Ministry
of Education. Valid and reliable measures of student learning outcomes are
important components in the drive for improvement in teaching and learning
process. Improvement of student learning achievement depends among other
factors on effective implementation of education policies at classroom level.
Accordingly, in a bid to improve the quality of education and to advance Rwanda's
agenda to become knowledge-based economy, the ministry of Education initiated
some strategies to ensure that effective teaching and learning take place and
due competences are acquired by learners. Among the strategies employed is
conducting the national examinations at the end of each cycle; Primary, Lower
secondary, Upper secondary, year three of TTC and level five of TVET.

Assessment of student learning (summative assessment) through administration
of national examinations is emphasized by the Presidential Order No 121/01 of
15/10/2020 establishing National Examination and School Inspection Authority
(NESA) as part of the government's efforts to improve the quality of education in
Rwanda.

NESA has the onus of overseeing and regulating national examinations. It is also
responsible for developing and administering national examinations, such as end
of term II exams, the Primary Leaving Examination (PLE), the Ordinary level national
examinations, the Advanced Level national exams, and TTC national exams as
well as ensuring that schools are providing quality education to students by
conducting regular inspections to assess their performance and adherence to
the standards. In fact, national examinations as provided for in the 2015 National
Curriculum framework which recognizes competency-based assessment as
a means of ensuring that students acquire the necessary skills, knowledge,
attitudes, and values to succeed in the modern world. The curriculum frame work
also places emphasis on alignment of the examinations with the content and
the learning outcomes described in the subject syllabi, as well as putting special
attention to standardization of scores and grading.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  1
National examinations characterise most educational systems the world over
and are the basis for awarding certificates to students who have completed a
learning cycle as well as selection and orientation of students for studies at a
higher level. National examinations act as a yardstick that enables teachers, School
leaders, and parents and other education stakeholders to judge the attainment
of curriculum learning outcomes as well as effectiveness of its implementation.

In Rwanda, national examinations are taken by candidates at the end of the
primary level, lower secondary level and upper secondary level of Basic Education,
Teacher Training College (TTC), and Technical and Vocational Education and
Training (TVET). The number of examinable subjects in national examinations for
each learning cycle are as follows: five (5) at Primary, nine (9) for lower secondary;
fourteen (14) for upper secondary; eighteen (18) for TTC and two hundred sixteen
(216) for TVET. In actuality, the analysis of national examination results is in line
with the National Strategy for Transformation (NST) in Rwanda, which emphasizes
the importance of quality education as a key driver for the country's development.
The strategy identifies education as a priority sector and highlights the need to
improve the quality of teaching and learning process.

Moreover, the initiative of analyzing national examinations for primary six, lower
secondary level, upper secondary level, TTCs and TVET has been inspired by other
countries in the region such as Kenya, Uganda, Ghana and South Africa where
national examinations analysis is conducted and elicits valuable information
for informed decision making by policy makers as well as informing education
supervisors at various levels.

An overview of the old national examinations grading system
in Rwanda

Before 1998, grading of candidate results in national examinations was based
on percentages which relied on the 100-point scale (0-100). From 2007 to 2021,
the grading system was changed to numerical grades (1 ­ 9), where 1 is the
highest and 9 the lowest. In this kind of grading sytem, raw data (marks) were
being converted into numerical grades as follows: Grades 1 and 2 are passes
with distinctions; grades 3,4,5 and 6 are passes with credit; grades 7 and 8 are
merely passes while; grade 9 indicates Unclassified or a Fail. The grades obtained
by a candidate in all the subjects are added to form grade aggregate. Annex 1
provides a summary of the achievement description, percentage score ranges,
grades and value used from 2007 to 2021 school year.

2  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
Revised national examination grading system

The grading consists of a 7-point scale expressed in both numerical and letter
grades reflecting the candidate's level of achievement in each subject. The scale
has 7 descriptors: `Excellent', `Very good', `Good', `Satisfactory'; `Adequate'; `Fair'
and `Fail'. The new harmonized and simplified grading indicates the achievement
descriptions for each level of achievement, the grade boundary, the associated
letter grades (A, B, C, D, E, S, F) and numerical grades (6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1, 0). Annex
2 summarizes grades ranges corresponding grades letters, Grades Values, and
description used in 2022.

Subsidiary subjects are graded by S or F, with S ranging from 40 Marks to 100
Marks and F ranging from 0 marks to 39 marks. The letter grade S has numerical
value of 1 and the letter grade F has numerical value 0. TVET and Professional
practical examinations, Integrated Assessments, Teaching Methods and Practice,
Accounting practice and Clinical attachment and practice are graded by A
ranging from 70 Marks to 100 Marks and F ranging from 0 to 69 marks. The letter
grade A has numerical value of 6 and the letter grade F has numerical value
0. However, NESA reserves the right to review grade boundaries when deemed
necessary.

At primary, there are 5 examinable subjects that are graded in national
examinations and only the grade value for each subject is considered (0 to 6).
The minimum aggregate is 0 and the maximum aggregate is 30. At O-level, there
are 9 examinable subjects. Only grade value for each subject is considered (0 to
6) and the minimum aggregate is 0 while the maximum aggregate is 54. With
respect to advanced level national examination (A-level for basic education and
TTCs), the grading is done based on combination and options. Both grade letter
and grade value are considered in addition to subject weighting. The minimum
aggregate is 0 while the maximum aggregate is 60.

In view of the above background, for the first time in Rwanda, the National
examinations and School Inspection Authority conducted an analysis of the
national examinations results to assess students' pass rate in 2022 based on the
new grading standards in comparison with the pass rate in previous years; 2019
and 2021. This will inform decision makers of the areas that need intervention to
improve students' performance in national examinations. The analysis will also
help to find out the achievement level of learners, thus tracking their strengths,
weaknesses, and gaps in the teaching and learning process in schools in Rwanda.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  3
1.2 Significance of national examination results analysis

The results from the analysis are important to the Government of Rwanda in
general, the MINEDUC, education policy implementing agencies including NESA,
REB, RTB, and other education stakeholders. In particular, the results will inform
NESA and other education sector actors of examinable subjects that need more
attention in teaching, learning and assessment.

The analysis of results will further guide NESA in improving Question Item Banks
(QIB) and implementation of assessments in reference to the assessment
standards and benchmarks. The results of this analysis will inspire research on
student performance and assessment to inform educational policy and practices.

The findings and recommendations from the analysis will serve as a basis
for decision makers and other education stakeholders to make their future
contribution in improving teaching, learning, assessment and ultimately student
performance. The feedback from this analysis will inspire teachers to identify the
underlying factors affecting students' performance and their role in enhancing
students' performance in their subjects.

1.3 Objectives of national examination results Analysis

The purpose of this analysis was to examine students' performance in National
Examinations in all examinable Subjects for basic education learning cycles (P6,
S3 & S6) and year 3 of TTC and TVET level 5 in the past three school years (2019,
2020/2021, and 2021/2022).

Specific objectives:

The following were the specific objectives of the study:

     1. To compare the pass rate of students in the old and the revised grading
          system.

     2. To identify subjects that require intervention in terms of teaching, learning
          & assessment to improve student's performance in the future.

1.4 Scope of the analysis

This analysis focused exclusively on students' performance in all the examinable
subjects in national examinations at the end of the learning cycles in basic
education and year 3 in TTCs in three school years (2019, 2021 and 2022).

4  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
SECTION TWO: METHODOLOGY

This section presents the procedure of data analysis, sources of data, methods
and tools of data collection, processing and interpretation, and data presentation.
The process of analyzing students' results in national exams began with collection
of students' results in national examinations for the three years (2019, 2021, and
2022) and organizing them. Data entry into SPSS version 20, Stata 14, Access,
and excel was done for easy tabulation, analysis and interpretation. Students'
results were processed, categorized, coded, and analyzed. Descriptive statistics
(measures of central tendency and variability) were used to analyse and interpret
the findings. Charts, frequency tables, and cross tabulation were generated using
SPSS version 20, Stata 14, Access and excel.

Students' results were retrieved from NESA examination data base, national
examination guidelines chief markers' reports produced during marking of national
examinations, and teachers during focus group discussions regarding national
examination performance. Data on student performance was categorized and
presented according to school ownership (private, government aided and public
schools); gender; school location (districts and provinces, rural and urban); and
school status (boarding and day schools).

It should be noted that while interpretation of standard deviation is dependent
upon the context and nature of dataset, in this report, interpretation of the standard
deviations and variation coefficients of scores for various subjects were based on
the American Psychological Association (APA)1 guidelines for reporting statistical
results, which recommends that a standard deviation that is less than one-third
of the mean can be considered a small variation, while a standard deviation
that is greater than or equal to one-third of the mean can be considered a large
variation.

1 American Psychological Association. (2020). Publication manual of the American Psychological
Association (7th ed.). https://doi.org/10.1037/0000165-000

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  5
SECTION THREE:
FINDINGS AND INTERPRETATION

This section presents the findings of the national examination results analysis for
basic education learning cycles (P6, S3, & S6), year 3 of TTCs and TVET for 2019,
2021 and 2022. According to Rwanda's national curriculum framework, each cycle
has a specific number of subjects that are examinable in national examinations.
At primary school level there are 5 subjects while at O-level there are 9 subjects.
At A-level 14 subjects are examinable and they are distributed in 15 combinations
out of which a student pursues a four-subject combination plus a subsidiary.
Similarly, in TTCs, there are 18 subjects distributed in 4 options. With respect to
TVET, analysis was done in 34 trades and each had 6-10 subjects in which students
were examined at national level over the three years. In 2022, one more trade with
17 subjects was introduced. In addition, exam results of 6 cross cutting subjects
were also analyzed.

3.1. Analysis of National Primary examination Results for
     2019, 2021 and 2022

This section presents findings from the analysis of primary examination results of
each examinable subjects. It highlights the general pass rate and performance,
pass rate by gender, by location (district, province, and rural-urban divide), by
school ownership for three years (2019, 2021 and 2022). The grades upon which
the pass rates were determined based on the old and new grading system are in
Annex 1 (old grading system) & Annex 2 (revised grading system).

in rural schools than to schools in urban settings. Further investigation and
interventions are needed to address these disparities and improve educational
outcomes for pupils in both urban and rural areas.

remained relatively high over the three years.

3.1.17 Pass rate in Primary SET subject

The pass rate of pupils' in in Primary SET national exams was analyzed and
the figure below shows the overall pass rate and by gender for the three years
considered.

Figure 21: General pass rate trend in SET and pass rate by gender

Figure 21 shows the pass rate trends in Science and Elementary Technology
(SET) in 2019, 2021 and 2022. In 2019, the pass rate in SET was 87.49%, indicating a
high level of proficiency in science and elementary technology subjects among
pupils. However, in 2021, the pass rate significantly reduced to 72.36%, indicating

22  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
a substantial drop in SET proficiency. This decline in pass rate could be to the
disruptions of learning caused by the COVID-19 pandemic. The pandemic led
to closure of schools and a shift to online learning, which may not have been
accessed by all pupils.

In 22022, the pass rate in SET rose to 86.81%, indicating improvement in SET
proficiency, but still below the 2019pass rate. This means that more effort is needed
to fully recover from the disruptions caused by the pandemic.

In terms of gender, there was generally no significant difference in pass rate
between boys and girls across the three school years considered in the analysis.
For instance, in 2019, the pass rate for girls (88.1%) was slightly more than that
of boys, which stood at 86.8%. Similarly, in 2021, the pass rates for girls and boys
were 71.4% and 73.5% respectively. In 2022, the pass rate for boys and girls was the
same (86.8%).

Overall, the data suggest that the COVID-19 pandemic had a significant impact
on children's pass rate in SET in Rwanda, with a notable decline in 2021. The
improvement seen in 2022 suggests that teachers have made relentless efforts
to mitigate the negative concomitants of the pandemic impact of the pandemic,
but there is still need for improvement.

3.1.18 Mean and variability of scores in P.6 SET exam

Determining the mean and standard deviation for student exam results is
important because it provides valuable information about the distribution of
scores and the general performance of the student. The table below provides
the mean, standard deviation and Variation Coefficient in SET national exams for
three years.

Table 5: Mean, SD and variability of scores in SET Subject

Mean                        2019     2021                   2022

Variation Coefficient (VC)  15.24    19                     21.85
                            65       86.88                  64.73
Number of pupils            281,586  250387                 227667

The mean score for SET in 2019 was 23.45, indicating that on average, student
performed relatively well, but in 2021, the mean score declined to 21.86, indicating
a drop in the average performance of pupils in SET. However, in2022, the mean
score improved significantly to 33.75, indicating a significant recovery in the
average performance of pupils in SET.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  23
With respect to Standard Deviation (SD), in 2019, the SD was 15.24, indicating that
the scores were relatively clustered around the mean. In 2021, the SD increased to
19, indicating greater variability in scores while in 2022, the SD increased further to
21.85, suggesting that the distribution of scores was more spread out.

The variation coefficient (VC) is a measure of relative variability, calculated as the
SD divided by the mean multiplied by 100. In 2019, the VC for SET was 65, indicating
a moderate level of variability. In 2021, the VC increased significantly to 86.88,
indicating a high level of variability. In 2022, the VC decreased to 64.73, indicating
a decrease in the relative variability of scores. The number of pupils (N) taking
SET in Rwanda was 281,586 in 2019, 250,387 in 2020/2021, and 227,667 in 2022. This
decrease in the number of pupils taking SET may be due to the disruptions caused
by the COVID-19 pandemic. Overall, the data suggests that there was a decline
in the average p of pupils in SET in 2021, likely due to the disruption caused by the
pandemic. However, there was a significant improvement in2022, although there
was also an increase in variability in scores.

Figure 22: Distribution of Scores in SET Subject

As indicated in Figure 22, in 2019, the majority of pupils scored between 0 and 30,
with very few scores above 50 while in 2021, the distribution shifted slightly to the
right, with more pupils scoring between 5 and 40.

In 2021/2022, there was a noticeable shift in the distribution, with more pupils
scoring in the range of 10 to 50. The distribution also appeared to have a flatter
curve, indicating that there was less variability in scores among pupils. Despite
this shift, there were still very few scores above 50, suggesting that the SET subject
is a challenging subject for many pupils.

Overall, the distribution of scores in the SET subject appears to be improving over
time, with fewer pupils scoring in the lower range and more pupils scoring in the
mid-range. The Chief markers of SET examination paper report shows that the
performance of pupils in 2022 National examinations was due to the Language
barrier, Lack of teaching materials and family poverty.

pass rate was recorded in the South province (90.9%), while the lowest pass rate
was for Kigali city (86.6%). This indicates that Mathematics pass rate continued to
improve across the provinces and Kigali city.

Overall, the data shows that the pass rate in Mathematics improved remarkably
across the provinces in Rwanda over the past three years. The Southern province
consistently had the highest pass rate, while Kigali city consistently had the
lowest pass rate. This suggests that measures were taken to improve teaching
and learning of Mathematics though more efforts are needed to ensure that all
the provinces achieve similar levels of pass rate.

Table 15 shows the average score, standard deviation, variation coefficient, and
the number of students for the Entrepreneurship subject in three different years:

The mean score for the Entrepreneurship subject significantly improved over the
years. In 2019, the mean score was 24.36, while in 2021, it increased to 39.18, and in

in Entrepreneurship over the years. In 2019, the SD was 19.56, and it increased to

scores from the mean was higher in 2021 than in 2019 and 2022. In 2019, the VC
was 80.30%, and it reduced to 60.21% in 2021 and further dropped to 57.24% in
2022. This implies that the variability of scores reduced over the years, and the
performance of students became more consistent.

Overall, the analysis suggests that there was a significant improvement in the
performance of students in Entrepreneurship subject over the years, with an
increase in mean scores, a decrease in the relative variability of scores, and a
reduction in the dispersion of scores from the mean. Efforts to sustain and improve
the performance in the Entrepreneurship subject should be explored such that
they help students to acquire relevant skills for entrepreneurship and prepare
them for future careers.

The mean score for the English subject has increased consistently from 26.63 in

in scores from the mean as indicated by the high SD and VC. According to
the American Psychological Association (APA) (2019) guidelines for reporting
statistical results, a standard deviation that is less than one-third of the mean
can be considered a small variation, while a standard deviation that is greater
than or equal to one-third of the mean can be considered a large variation.

Overall, the data suggests an improvement in the students' performance in the
English subject over the years, with an increase in the mean score and a decrease
in the variation coefficient. However, the increase in the standard deviation
suggests that there was also a wider range of performance among students in
the later years.

2019 and 92.45% in 2022. In English, the pass rate has steadily increased from 69.19%
in 2019 to 98.07% in 2022, indicating a consistent improvement in performance.
Kinyarwanda has maintained a consistently high pass rate, with 94.18% in 2019,
87.23% in 2021, and 99.64% in 2022, indicating a high level of proficiency in the
subject.

Overall, the performance trend in all S3 subjects shows a consistent improvement
in performance over the years, with some subjects showing a remarkable
improvement in pass rates, while others maintain a consistently high pass rate.

male students was 82.5% and for female it stood at 79.8%. This suggests that both
male and female students improved their performance in French, although the
pass rate for male students was slightly higher than female students.

3.4. Analysis of TTCs national examination results for 2019,
     2021 and 2022

This section presents results of analysis of national examinations for TTCs for

rates per subject. The subjects are: Foundations of education, Foundations of
Early Childhood education, Mathematics, French, social and religious studies,
English, Creative performance and Physical education, Kinyarwanda, Integrated
Science, Numeracy &Mathematics, General science &Mathematics, Kiswahili,
Entrepreneurship, and Teaching practice. Different from the previous years,
in 2022 new subjects were examined and these include: Biology, Economics,
Physics, Chemistry, Social and Religious Studies, Geography, History, Creative
Performance, and teaching methods.

over the three years, from 14.9 in 2019 to 15.6 in 2021, and then to 17.5 in 2022. The
variation coefficient (VC), which is the ratio of the standard deviation to the mean
expressed as a percentage, decreased from 24.7 in 2019 to 20.7 in 2021, and then
increased to 33.7 in 2022. This suggests that the variability in mathematics scores
increased in 2022. The number of students (N) was 1,161 in 2019, decreased to 981
in 2021, and then increased to 1,386 in 2022. Overall, the analysis shows that there
have been changes in the mean, standard deviation, and variation coefficient of
mathematics scores over the three school years indicating variations in student
performance. Specifically, the standard deviation increased from 14.9 in 2019 to
17.5 in 2022 implying that there are students with very low scores and others with
scores tending to 100% as further illustrated in the figure below.

Figure 150:Distribution of Scores in Mathematics

Figure150 shows that the distribution of scores in mathematics subject over three
school years (2019, 2021, and 2022) was symmetrical in 2019 with the majority of
students' scores were concentrated around the mean score with a few in the
extremes. This suggests that the majority of students were consistent. Nonetheless,
in 2021 the curve is skewed towards the left (asymmetrical) with majority of scores
concentrated on the right. In 2022, the distribution of scores was somewhat
symmetrical with scores evenly spread to both sides of the curve. Overall, the
distribution of scores in mathematics subject over the three schoolyears shows
that there were changes in the performance levels of the students taking the
exam.

in Kiswahili subject over the years. However, there is still a gap on the side of
female students whereby the pass rate is not yet 100%.

in the two years, with female students performing almost similarly to male

better understanding of the subject. In conclusion, the performance by gender in

a reduction in the gender gap in pass rates in the last two years.

Table 48 provides the mean score, standard deviation, variation coefficient, and
the number of students for each nationally examined subject at TTC in 2022. The
mean score for each subject ranges from 40.5 for chemistry to 93.7 for Religious
Education, indicating that the performance of students varies significantly across
subjects. A high SD indicates that the data is widely spread out from the mean,
while a low SD indicates that the data is tightly clustered around the mean. In this
case, Chemistry has the highest SD of 16.1, indicating that there is a relatively large
amount of variation in student performance in this subject. On the other hand,
Religious Education has the lowest SD of 5.6, indicating that student performance
in this subject is relatively consistent. The variation coefficient (VC) is another
measure of variability, calculated as the ratio of the standard deviation to the
mean, expressed as a percentage. A high VC indicates that the data has a high
degree of variability relative to the mean, while a low VC indicates that the data
has low variability relative to the mean. In this case, chemistry has the highest VC
of 39.8%, indicating that there is a relatively high degree of variability in student
performance in this subject relative to the mean score of 40.5. On the other hand,
Religious Education has the lowest VC of 6.0%, indicating that there is relatively
low variability in student performance in this subject relative to the mean score
of 93.7. It is also important to note that the number of students varies across
subjects depending on the subject option/ combination pursued by a student.
Overall, the data in the table suggests that there is a significant variation in student

the other topics but still showed a significant improvement from 22.97% in 2019 to
93.76% in 2022.

Practical ACC had a pass rate of almost 100% in all three years, indicating that
students have a good grasp of practical accounting concepts, which could be
due to the hands-on nature of the course.

Table 58 shows the mean, standard deviation, and variation coefficient (VC) of
different subjects of Automotive Electricity and Electronics Systems (AES) trade
for the years 2019, 2021, and 2022. For the AES related to automotive electrical
starting and charging system, its observed on the table that the mean was 58.00
in 2019, decreased to 40.40 in 2021 but increased tremendously to 79.40 in 2022.
The standard deviation was 17.09 in 2019, 20.11 in 2021 and 14.13 in 2022. The variation
coefficient decreased from 29.47 in 2019, to 49.78 in 2021 and 17.79 in 2022. This
indicates that the data became less variable in the later years, and the values
were closer to the mean. Similarly, for the AES related to vehicle drive assistance
and electronic system, the mean decreased from 56.50 in 2019 to 38.25 in 2021
and after increased significantly to 80.58 in 2022. The standard deviation also
increased from 16.57 in 2019 to 20.69 in 2021 and after went down to 15.70 in 2022.
However, the variation coefficient increased from 29.33 in 2019 to 54.07 in 2021
and decreased to 19.48 in 2022. This suggests that the variability of the student
scores increased, but not as much as the mean. For the AES related to automotive
engine and auxiliaries systems, the mean decreased from 56.38 in 2019 to 19.40 in
2021 and in 2022 went up to 64.87. The standard deviation increased from 15.82 in
2019 to 23.71 in 2021 and a slight decrease to 19.41 in 2022. The variation coefficient
increased significantly from 28.06 in 2019 to 122.24 in 2021 before going down to
29.92 in 2022. This indicates that the student scores became more variable in
the later years, and the values were further from the mean. For the AES related
to vehicle electronics stability and signaling system, the mean decreased from
64.23 in 2019 to 23.22 in 2021 and increased up to 79.20 in 2022. The standard
deviation slightly increased from 19.30 in 2019 to 21.11 in 2021 and decreased to
16.03 in 2022. The variation coefficient increased from 30.05 in 2019 to 90.89 in 2021
and decreased significantly back to 20.24 in 2022. This suggests that the student
scores became less variable in the later years, and the values were closer to the
mean. For the AES related to automotive engine ignition and powering, the mean
decreased from 72.76 in 2019 to 22.17 in 2021 and increased in 2022 to 78.69. The
standard deviation increased from 13.48 in 2019 to 24.97 in 2021 and decreased to
16.25 in 2022. The variation coefficient increased significantly from 18.52 in 2019 to
112.62 in 2021 before going down to 20.65 in 2022. This indicates that the student
scores became more variable in 2021, and the values were further from the mean.
Finally, for the AES related to practical AES, the mean decreased significantly from
92.32 in 2019 to 25.83 in 2021 and went back up to 92.80 in 2022. The standard
deviation increased from 6.11 in 2019 to 23.16 in 2021 and decreased to 4.06 in 2022.
The variation coefficient increased significantly from 6.62 in 2019 to 89.66 in 2021
and went back down to 4.37 in 2022. This suggests that the student scores became
more variable in 2019. Overall, it appears that there is some variability in the AES
trade pass rate data over the three years, with some AES subjects having higher
mean values or more variability than others. However, without further context or
analysis, it is difficult to draw any conclusions about the causes or implications of
these variations.

Analysis of pass rate in BUS by school ownership shows that for Business
management, the highest pass rates were seen in the PU schools, with 82.4%,
100%, and 100% in the three respective years. GA schools had lower pass rates,
but still saw high improvement in 2021 and 2022 with pass rates of 98.3% and
100%, respectively. PR schools had the lowest pass rates, with only 60% passing
in 2019 and improving to 72.7% and 100% in the later years. For Stock and cash
management, the PU schools had the highest pass rates in all three years, with
17.65%, 100%, and 100%, respectively. GA schools had the lowest pass rates but
improved drastically from 19.4% in 2019 to 96.5% in 2021 and 100% in 2022. PR
schools only had data for 2021 and 2022 but had a 100% pass rate in both years.
For Bank account records and monetary transactions, the high pass rates were
seen in the GA schools in all three years, with 54.84%, 99.1%, and 100%. PU schools
had highest pass rates, with 82.35%, 100%, and 100% in the respective years. PR
schools had no data for 2019 but had pass rates of 72.7% and 88.24% in 2021 and
2022, respectively.

For Customer services coordination and delivery, the highest pass rates were
seen in the PU schools, with 94.12%, 100%, and 100% in the three respective years.
GA schools had high pass rates as well, with 90.32%, 99.1%, and 100%. PR schools
had lower pass rates, but still improved from 80% in 2019 to 100% in 2022. For Taxes
computation commission and market study, the highest pass rates were seen
in the PU schools, with 94.12%, 100%, and 100%. GA schools had lower pass rates,
with 59.68%, 99.1%, and 100%. PR schools had no data for 2019, but had pass rates
of 100% in 2021 and 2022. For Practical BUS, all schools had very high pass rates
in all three years, with PU schools having 100% pass rates in all years, GA schools
having 100% pass rates in 2019 and 2022 and 99.1% in 2021, and PR schools having
100% pass rates in all years. In general PU schools tended to have the highest pass
rates across all subjects in all years, followed by GA schools, and then PR schools
with the lowest pass rates. However, all schools showed improvements in pass
rates over time.

the least in 2019 and was recorded the second bottom in 2021. This highlights
the need for efforts in this subject. Students' performance in Taxation Operations
subject increased from 52.43% in 2019 to 81.7% in 2021 and further to an impressive
100% pass rate in 2022, making it the only subject passed by all students in that
year. The performance in this subject shows positive improvement that could be
due to efforts invested in it by different concerned parties.

Students' performance in Financial Mathematics subject consistently has the
lowest passing rate across all three years, hitting the lowest pass rate in all CTO
subjects in 2019 at 30.1% then increased to 67.7% in 2021 and further to 84.75% in 2022
yet being the least succeeded course in the two last academic years. This indicates
the existence of challenges in this subject and the need for resolutions. Students'
performance in Customs law and cleaning procedures subject increased from
69.9% to 91.4% and further to 98.3% in 2019, 2021 and 2022 respectively. Students'
performance in this subject demonstrates a consistent positive trend as time goes
on. Students' performance in Cost Accounting subject also recorded a positive
trend as it improved from 36.89% in 2019 to 79.6% and extended to 87.93% in 2021
and 2022 respectively. However, comparing the performance of students in this
subject to other subjects, it is observed that it still lags behind other courses and
therefore calls for attention as well. Students' performance in Tax audit subject
recorded a positive tendency as well, increasing from 56.31% to 83.9% and further
to 96.61% in 2019, 2021 and 2022 respectively. Students' performance in Freight
forward operation and tariff classification subject shows that it is the second most
mastered subject by students after practical CTO, recording a positive trendy as
well from 70.87% to 93.5% and to 98.31% in 2019, 2021 and 2022 respectively. This
indicates that the right things are being done in this subject leading to students
consistently performing well in this course. Practical CTO subject has the highest
passing rate in the first two years, with 86.41% pass rate in 2019, and 100% of
students passing in 2021, a slight decrease was observed in 2022 when 95% of
students passed the National Exam.

As a general observation, some subjects such as Customs Law and Clearing
Procedures, Tax Audit, and Freight Forward Operation and Tariff Classification, have
consistently high passing rates across all three years. However, other subjects,
such as corporate accounting, Financial Accounting and Cost Accounting, show
a notable improvement in the percentage of students passing in 2022 compared
to the previous years. In conclusion, the table suggests an overall improvement
in the performance of CTO students over the years. However, there are significant
variations in the performance of different subjects, with some consistently
outperforming others. The analysis of the table can provide valuable insights for
instructors and policymakers to identify areas that require improvement and

200  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
implement targeted interventions to enhance the quality of education in Customs
and Taxation Operations trade.

Table 92 provides details the pass rates and frequencies by gender for each
subject under the trade of Customs and Taxation Operations (CTO) in the 2019,
2021, and 2022 national examinations. For financial Accounting, in 2019, the pass
rate for females was 46.48%, while for males, it was 40.63%. In 2021, the pass rate
increased significantly for both females (79.69%) and males (81.25%). In 2022,
the pass rate continued to increase, with a 92.75% pass rate for females and a
97.96% pass rate for males. In this subject, no gender dominated the other. For

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  201
economic Environment, in 2019, the pass rate for females was 47.89%, while for
males, it was 53.13%. In 2021, the pass rate increased for both females (78.13%)
and males (97.92%). In 2022, the pass rate for females continued to increase up
to 97.1%, while the pass rate for males reached 100%. In this subject male student
outperformed their female colleagues across the studied years. For Corporate
Accounting, in 2019, the pass rate for females was 25.35%, while for males, it was
28.13%. In 2021, the pass rate significantly increased for both females (73.44%) and
males (75.00%). In 2022, the pass rate continued to increase, with a 94.2% pass
rate for females and a 95.92% pass rate for males. In this subject male student
consistently performed better than female students. Analyzing the performance
in the taxation Operations subject, in 2019, the pass rate for females was 49.30%,
while for males, it was 59.38%. In 2021, the pass rate significantly increased for
both females (81.25%) and males (85.42%). In 2022, the pass rate for females and
males both increased to 100% indicating that in the final year, both genders had
mastered the content of this subject at the same level. For Financial Mathematics,
in 2019, the pass rate for females was 23.94%, while for males, it was 43.75%. In
2021, the pass rate significantly increased for both females (64.06%) and males
(79.17%). In 2022, the pass rate continued to increase, with an 81.16% pass rate for
females and a 89.8% pass rate for males. Once again, males outperformed their
female counterparts. For Customs Law and Clearing Procedures, in 2019, the pass
rate for females was 64.79%, while for males, it was 81.25%. In 2021, the pass rate
for females was 87.5%, while for males, it was 93.75%. In 2022, the pass rate for
females increased to 97.1%, while for males, it remained at 100%.

In the subject of Customs law and clearing procedures, both male and female
students have shown an improvement in their performance over the years. Male
students have consistently performed better than female students in this subject.
Females pass rate grew from 64.79% in 2019 to 87.50% in 2021 increasing to 97.1%
in 2022, while male students started with a pass rate of 81.25% in 2019, increased
to 93.75% and improved to a perfect pass rate of 100% in 2022.

For the subject of Cost accounting, both male and female students have also
shown an improvement in their performance over the years. However, female
students performed better than male students in this subject, with a pass rate of
40.85% in 2019 increasing to 73.44 in 2021 and further to 88.06% in 2022, while male
students started with a pass rate of 28.13% in 2019, improved to 87.50% in 2021 and
remained constant at 87.76% in 2022.

In the subject of Tax audit, both male and female students have again shown an
improvement in their performance over the years. Male students did better than
their female colleagues in this subject, with a pass rate of 56.34% in 2019 increasing
to76.56% in 2021 and further to 94.2% in 2022, while male students started with a
pass rate of 56.25% in 2019, increased to 89.58% in 2021 and improved to a perfect
pass rate of 100% in 2022.

In the subject of Freight forward operation and tariff classification, both male and
female students have shown an improvement in their performance over the years.

202  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
Both genders performed almost at the same level in this subject, with female's
pass rate of 70.42% in 2019 increasing to 93.75% in 2021 and further to 97.1% in
2022, while male students started with a pass rate of 71.88% in 2019 which grew to
93.75% (same pass rate as their female colleagues) in 2021 and also improved to
a perfect pass rate of 100% in 2022.

Finally, in the subject of Practical CTO, both male and female students have shown
a consistently high level of performance over the years, with female students
starting with a pass rate of 87.32% in 2019, hitting a perfect pass rate of 100% in
2021 and slightly decreasing to 91.55% in 2022. Male students also started with a
high pass rate of 84.38 in 2019 and improved to the full pass rate of 100% in 2021
which was maintained in 2022.

Overall, there were significant improvements in the pass rates for both females
and males in most subjects between 2019 and 2022, with most subjects achieving
a pass rate of 90% or higher in the last year. While females showed a slight dip in
2021 before recovering in 2022, they also tended to have slightly lower pass rates
than males in most subjects, the difference was not substantial.

Table 93 provides information on the average score variation coefficients trend
in the trade of Customs and Taxation Operations (CTO) across three years: 2019,
2021, and 2022. The variation coefficient (VC) indicates the degree of variation
or dispersion in a set of data relative to the mean. A higher VC suggests greater
variability or spread in the data, while a lower VC indicates more consistency or
uniformity. Looking at the table, we can observe that the mean scores for most
of the CTO courses have increased from 2019 to 2021 but slightly decreased in
2022. The exceptions are Economic environment, corporate accounting, Tax
audit, Freight forward operation and tariff classification and Practical CTO course,
which has seen a consistent though relatively low increase in mean scores across
the three years. The variation coefficients have varied across the different CTO
courses and years. In some courses, such as Corporate Accounting and Taxation
Operations, the variation coefficient kept on decreasing across the three years,
indicating lesser variability and more consistency in scores. Interestingly, some
courses have shown a fluctuating behavior, recording a significant decrease in
the variation coefficient from 2019 to 2021 and a slight increase in 2022, these
include Economic Environment, Customs law and clearing procedures, Cost
accounting, Tax audit, Freight forward operation and tariff classification as well as
Practical CTO. This could be attributed to the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic,
which may have led to a more standardized teaching and assessment approach.
However, in other courses like Financial Accounting and Financial Mathematics,
the variation coefficient has increased from 2019 to 2021, suggesting greater
variability in scores. In summary, the table shows that while the mean scores
have varied across the different CTO courses and years, the trend in the variation
coefficient is not consistent. Some courses have become more consistent in their
scores, while others have become more variable. The reasons for these variations
are unclear and may require further investigation.

trend of students' performance in food preparations also exhibited a similar
trend, with a sharp increase in 2021 from the recorded pass rate of 17.77% to 82.9%
in 2021, followed by a modest increase to 93.86% in the year 2022.This suggests
that students faced difficulties in understanding and applying the principles of
food preparation in 2019, which were addressed in the subsequent academic
year. Moreover, students' performance in Principles of catering and stock control
subject increased notably from 69.52% in 2019 to 82.3% in 2021 and further to
93.29% in 2022, indicating a significant improvement in students understanding
of this subject. The percentage of students' passing in cooking methods and
basic dishes subject also showed a steady increase over the three years from
78.24.5 in 2019 to 85.5.% in 2021 and then to 97.96% in 2022 academic year.

significantly over the three years with the lowest record of 42% pass rate in 2019
changing into an interesting 82.4% in 2021 and further growing to 96.18% in 2022,

206  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
indicating a notable improvement in their understanding and application of food
storage and safety principles in the culinary arts trade.

to all other subjects, indicating that students have gained strong practical skills
from this course. The pass rate in this subject increased from 97.88% in 2019 to
100% in 2022

food preparations, where the percentage of students who passed increased
from 17.77% in 2019 to 93.86% in 2022. This is followed by food storage and safety,
where the percentage of students who passed increased from 42.62% in 2019 to
96.18% in 2022. The subjects with the highest pass rates across all three years
were practical CUA, coaching section staff on job skills, and cooking methods and
basic dishes. In 2019, the highest performing subject was "Practical CUA" with a
pass rate of 97.88%, followed by "Coaching section staff on job skills" at 80.9%. The
lowest performing subject was "Food preparations" with a pass rate of only 17.77%.

In 2021, a significant improvement in the performance of all subjects, with
"Coaching section staff on job skills" and "Food preparations" both showing pass
rates of over 82%. "Practical CUA" remained the highest performing subject with
a pass rate of 99%. In 2022, the trend of improvement continued, with all subjects
showing pass rates of over 90%, and "Practical CUA" achieving a perfect score of
100%. In conclusion, we can see that there was a consistent improvement in student
performance across all subjects in the trade of Culinary Arts, with "Practical CUA"
being the strongest performing subject throughout the three years.

noting that the subjects tested in the National Exams are practical and hands-on,

Table 103 presents the mean score, standard deviation (SD), and variation
coefficient (VC) of students' performance in different subjects under the trade
of Electronic Services (ELS) across three years: 2019, 2021, and 2022. The mean
score represents the average performance of the students in each subject, while
the standard deviation measures how much the scores vary from the mean.
The variation coefficient is a relative measure of dispersion that shows the
standard deviation as a percentage of the mean. For the subject "Apply Basic
Microcontroller," the mean score of students increased from 34.84 in 2019 to 63.01
in 2021 and remained almost the same at 62.49 in 2022. The standard deviation
also increased from 15.49 in 2019 to 20.25 in 2021 and slightly decreased to 18.52 in
2022, indicating that the scores of students became less dispersed. The variation
coefficient also decreased over the years from 44.46 to 32.13 and down to 29.64
in 2019, 2021 and 2022 respectively, indicating a more consistent performance
of students as time goes on. For "Maintain Electronic Display," subject the mean
score increased from 52.02 in 2019 to 73.64 in 2021 and then experience a sharp
decrease to 50.29 in 2022. The standard deviation decreased over the years
from 19.23 to 17.81 and the 17.94 in 2019, 2021 and 2022 respectively. However, the
variation coefficient was inconsistent, starting at 36.97% in 2019, decreasing to
24.18 in 2021 but increasing again to 33.88 in 2022, indicating an inconsistency
in student marks dispersion. For "Fundamentals of Electronics," the mean score
increased from 31.83 in 2019 to 48.86 in 2021 and then slightly decreased to 47.22
in 2022. The standard deviation increased over the years from 15.81 to 18.83 and
further to 22.90 in 2019, 2021 and 2022 respectively, indicating that the scores
of students became more dispersed. The variation coefficient decreased from
49.66 in 2019 to 38.53 in 2021 but unfortunately increased again to 48.51 in 2022,
indicating a higher dispersion of students' performance. For "Install and repair
CCTV camera system," the mean score increased from 48.96 in 2019 to 61.79 in
2021 and then increased again to 72.13 in 2022. The standard deviation increased
from 16.61 in 2019 to 20.99 in 2021 and almost stayed the same at 20.33 in 2022
indicating that the scores of students became a little bit more dispersed over the
years. However, the variation coefficient remained relatively stable over the years

from 63.68 in 2019 to 36.18 in 2021 and then down to 27.36 in 2022, indicating an
increase in the dispersion of scores relative to the mean. For Processing Financial
Transactions, we can see that there is a slight increase in the mean score from
27.04 in 2019 to 47.20 in 2021 and then to 51.70 in 2022. Unfortunately, the standard
deviation also increased from 13.78 in 2019 to 17.12 in 2021 and then to 23.41 in 2022.
We can also observe a discrepancy in the variation coefficient which decreased
to 36.27 in 2021 from 50.98 in 2019 and then suddenly increased to 45.28 in 2022,
indicating a relatively high dispersion of scores relative to the mean. For Planning
a Catering, we can see that there was a significant increase in the mean score
from 38.41 in 2019 to 61.39 in 2021 and then to 76.05 in 2022. The standard deviation
decreased from 20.00 in 2019 to 19.31 in 2021 and then further down to 17.88 in
2022, indicating a reduction in the variation of scores. The variation coefficient
also decreased from 52.07 recorded in 2019 to 31.46 in 2021 and then went down
again to 23.51 in 2022, indicating a decrease in the dispersion of scores relative
to the mean. For Prepare and Provide Beverages, we can see that there was a
considerable increase in the mean score from 22.64 in 2019 to 56.14 in 2021 and
then to 65.95 in 2022. The standard deviation also increased from 15.72 in 2019
to22.61 in 2021 and then slightly decreased to 18.73 in 2022. On a Positive note, the
variation coefficient decreased from 69.43 in 2019 to 40.28 in 2021 and then further
down to 28.39 in 2022, indicating a relatively low dispersion of scores relative to
the mean. Looking at the data for "Practical FBS," we can see that the mean score
was highest in 2019 (94.35) and lowest in 2021 (90.92) but increased again in 2022
(94.33). The SD was lowest in 2022 (3.82) and highest in 2021 (5.40), while the SD
was recorded at 4.85 in 2019. The variation coefficient (VC) was also lowest in
2022 (4.05) and highest in 2021 (5.94) and it had recorded 5.14 in 2019 indicating
the relative variability of the scores. Overall, the data suggests that students in
Practical FBS have performed relatively consistently over the three years, with
a slight drop in performance in 2021 that was then recovered in 2022. The low
standard deviation and variation coefficient in the most recent year suggest that
scores were less dispersed and more consistent across the student population.

Basing on the data in the Table 112 it appears that the pass rate of male candidates
has decreased in six different examinable core subjects in FOB trade: Apply
technics and tactics of football player, developing a football player skill, perform
football rules and regulations, Player's physical fitness development, Preparing
football competition, and Practical FOB. In Apply techniques and tactics of football
player subject, the pass rate for male candidates decreased from 100% in 2019 to
85.3% in 2021. It's concerning to observe that there was also a significant decrease
in performance among male candidates in developing football player skills,
dropping from 96.3% in 2019 to 67.6% in 2021. In addition, the table also shows a
significant drop in the pass rate for male candidates in the Perform football rules
and regulations subject from 96.3% in 2019 to 87.9% in 2021. In Player's physical
fitness development, the pass rate for male candidates dropped from 100% in
2019 to 79.4% in 2021. Similarly, the pass rate for male candidates in Preparing
football competition also decreased from 100% in 2019 to 75.8% in 2021. Finally, the
pass rate for male candidates in Practical FOB dropped from 100% in 2019 to 87.5%
in 2021. It is possible that this subject requires a higher level of physical ability and
skill, which may have been more challenging for male candidates in 2021.

There could be various reasons for this decline, such as: Difficulty level: It is possible
that the difficulty level of the subject increased in 2021, leading to a lower pass
rate. This could be due to changes in the exam format, content, or scoring criteria.
Pandemic-related disruptions: The COVID-19 pandemic caused significant
disruptions in education, including delays in exams, reduced teaching hours,

226  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
and changes in teaching methods. These disruptions could have impacted male
candidates' ability to learn and perform well in different subjects. Other factors:
Other factors such as inadequate preparation, lack of interest, or personal issues
could have contributed to the decline in male candidates' pass rate. Overall, the
decrease in pass rates for male candidates in these subjects suggests that there
may be some factors that are hindering their ability to perform well. It would be
important to investigate these factors further to develop strategies to help male
candidates improve their performance in these subjects.

genders, but the data also suggests that there are positive trends of improvement
and consistent high performance in some subjects.

variation increased in 2021 but decreased in 2022, indicating a greater spread of
scores in 2021 but less variability in 2022.

In Tofu technology and soymilk processing; the mean score increased from 2019
to 2021 and further increased in 2022. The standard deviation and coefficient of
variation also decreased in 2021 and 2022, indicating less variability in performance.
In Hygiene, waste management, and food analysis; the mean score increased
from 2019 to 2021 but decreased in 2022. The standard deviation and coefficient
of variation increased in 2022, indicating a greater spread of scores and more
variability in performance. In Basics engineering sciences: The mean score
decreased from 2019 to 2022. The standard deviation and coefficient of variation
increased in 2021 and 2022, indicating greater variability in performance. In
Practical FOP; the mean score remained relatively stable over the three years but
there were fluctuations in the standard deviation and coefficient of variation. The
standard deviation decreased in 2021 and increased in 2022, while the coefficient
of variation decreased in 2021 and increased in 2022. It is important to note that
the provided data does not reveal any causal factors for these trends and further
analysis may be required to understand the underlying factors contributing to
these changes.

and variability of the scores. There are mixed trends in the performance of students
in different subjects related to forestry trade over the three years. Some subjects

and/or variation of coefficient, while others showed a decline in mean score and/
or an increase in standard deviation and/or variation of coefficient.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  239
The mean score for Soil conservation has been increasing steadily over the years.
It increased from 56.3 in 2019 to 61.5 in 2021 and then further increased to 69.3
in 2022. This suggests that there has been an improvement in Soil conservation
performance over the years. The standard deviation of the scores has been
fluctuating. It decreased from 13.8 in 2019 to 10.3 in 2021 and then increased to
16.1 in 2022. This suggests that there has been a greater spread of scores in 2022
compared to the previous year. The coefficient of variation (CV) is a measure
of relative variability, which is the ratio of the standard deviation to the mean. A
decreasing CV indicates a reduction in the variability of the scores. The CV for Soil
conservation decreased from 24.5 in 2019 to 16.8 in 2021, indicating a reduction in
the variability of scores. However, it increased to 23.2 in 2022, indicating that the
variability of scores has increased.

The mean score in Tree nursery and forest plantation establishment decreased
from 68.6 in 2019 to 58.5 in 2021, indicating a decline in performance during that
period. However, it increased to 63.1 in 2022, indicating some improvement from
the previous year. The standard deviation is a measure of the amount of variation
or dispersion in the data. The decrease in standard deviation from 15.8 in 2019 to
11.5 in 2021 indicates that there was less variation in the data during that period.
However, the standard deviation increased to 15.3 in 2022, indicating that there
was more variation in the data during that period compared to the previous year.
The coefficient of variation (CV) is a standardized measure of the dispersion of
a probability distribution. It is calculated as the ratio of the standard deviation to
the mean, expressed as a percentage. The decrease in the coefficient of variation
from 23.1 in 2019 to 19.7 in 2021 indicates that the variation in the data was relatively
smaller compared to the mean during that period. However, the coefficient of
variation increased to 24.2 in 2022, indicating that the variation in the data was
relatively larger compared to the mean during that period compared to the
previous year.

The mean score in Basic properties of wood Assessment increased consistently
over the past three years. The mean score increased from 59.2 in 2019 to 61.9 in 2021
and then increased further to 73.0 in 2022.The standard deviation of the scores,
which measures the spread or variability of the data, decreased from 17.0 in 2019
to 13.4 in 2021, and remained constant at 13.4 in 2022. This suggests that the scores
were less spread out in 2021 compared to 2019, and that the spread remained the
same in 2022 compared to 2021.The coefficient of variation (CV), which is the ratio
of the standard deviation to the mean expressed as a percentage, decreased
from 28.7 in 2019 to 21.7 in 2021, and then increased slightly to 18.4 in 2022. A lower
CV indicates that the data are less spread out around the mean, which suggests
greater consistency in the scores. The given data shows a decreasing trend in the
mean score of forest management plan implementation from 2019 to 2022, which
indicates a decline in the overall effectiveness of forest management practices.
Additionally, the standard deviation, which is a measure of the spread of data
around the mean, decreased in 2021 but increased in 2022, which suggests that

240  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
the data points were more clustered around the mean in the former year but
more dispersed in the latter year.

The coefficient of variation (CV), which is the ratio of the standard deviation to
the mean expressed as a percentage, decreased from 32.2 in 2019 to 22.2 in 2021,
indicating a reduction in the variability of the data. However, the CV increased to
35.6 in 2022, suggesting a higher degree of variability in the data in this period.
From the given information, we can see that there have been fluctuations in the
mean score, standard deviation, and coefficient of variation of forest protection
over the three years.The mean score decreased from 61.1 in 2019 to 56.6 in 2021
and then increased to 63.5 in 2022. This indicates that there was a decrease in
forest protection in 2021 compared to 2019, but it improved in 2022. The standard
deviation increased from 16.7 in 2019 to 11.9 in 2021 and then increased to 12.3 in
2022. This means that there was less variability in the scores in 2021 and 2022
compared to 2019.

The coefficient of variation decreased from 27.3 in 2019 to 21.0 in 2021 and then
decreased further to 19.4 in 2022. This indicates that the variability in the scores
decreased over time and the scores became more consistent. Based on the given
information, it seems that there has been a significant decrease in the mean score
for Forest exploitation from 2019 to 2022. The mean score decreased from 62.9 in
2019 to 65.7 in 2021 and then dropped to 45.5 in 2022. This indicates a considerable
decline in the quality of Forest exploitation in the last year. The standard deviation,
on the other hand, shows a mixed trend. It decreased from 14.4 in 2019 to 11.9 in
2021, indicating that the data was more tightly clustered around the mean in 2021
compared to 2019. However, it then increased to 13.7 in 2022, which may indicate
that the data points are more spread out from the mean in the most recent year.
The coefficient of variation, which is a measure of the relative variability of the
data, decreased from 22.8 in 2019 to 18.2 in 2021, indicating a decrease in the
relative variability of the data. However, it then increased to 30.1 in 2022, which
indicates that the relative variability of the data increased significantly in the last
year. These measures suggest that there has been a significant decline in the
quality of Forest exploitation, with a sharp decrease in mean score, an increase in
standard deviation, and an increase in the coefficient of variation in the last year.
It may be useful to investigate the causes of this decline and take measures to
address them. The mean score in Forest Engineering increased significantly from
50.7 in 2019 to 66.5 in 2021, which indicates an improvement in the performance
of the students. However, in the following year (2022), the mean score decreased
to 53.3, which suggests a decline in performance compared to the previous year.
The standard deviation is a measure of how spread out the data is, and it provides
information on the variability of the scores.

In Forest Engineering, the standard deviation decreased from 16.4 in 2019 to
12.4 in 2021, which indicates a decrease in the variability of the scores. However,
in the following year (2022), the standard deviation increased to 16.9, which
suggests an increase in the variability of the scores compared to the previous

year. The coefficient of variation (CV) is a relative measure of variability that is
useful for comparing data sets with different means and standard deviations.
The CV decreased from 32.3 in 2019 to 18.6 in 2021, which indicates a decrease
in the relative variability of the scores. However, in the following year (2022), the
CV increased to 31.6, which suggests an increase in the relative variability of the

93.62%.

the academic years 2019 and 2021. However, in the academic year 2022, the pass
rate dropped to 89.36% for GA schools and 87.5% for PU schools.

In conclusion, the table shows that both GA and PU schools have consistently high
pass rates in most of the courses, with only a few fluctuations in some courses.
Overall, the pass rates have been encouraging and suggest a positive trend in
the performance of the Forestry program over the years.

candidates have improved from 33.3% in 2019 to 50% in 2021 and 100% in 2022.
For male candidates, the percentage scores have consistently been high, with
50% in 2019, 71.4% in 2021, and 83.3% in 2022. For Fundamental of hydropower plant
operations, we can see that the percentage scores for both male and female
candidates have consistently been high, with 100% in all three years. For Install
and protect electrical circuits, we can see that the percentage scores for female
candidates have fluctuated from 66.7% in 2019 to 50% in 2021 and 100% in 2022. For
male candidates, the percentage scores have consistently been high, with 83.3%
in 2019, 85.7% in 2021, and 83.3% in 2022. For Pneumatic and hydraulic systems, we
can see that there were no female candidates in 2019, but in 2021 and 2022, the
percentage scores for female candidates were 100%. For male candidates, the
percentage scores have increased from 33.3% in 2019 to 64.3% in 2021 and 100%
in 2022.

For Technical and electrical drawing, we can see that the percentage scores for
female candidates have fluctuated from 66.7% in 2019 to 100% in 2021 and 2022.
For male candidates, the percentage scores have consistently been high, with
91.7% in 2019, 92.9% in 2021, and 100% in 2022. For Practical HPE, we can see that the
percentage scores for both male and female candidates have consistently been
high, with 100% in all three years. Overall, we can see that the percentage scores
for male candidates have consistently been high, while the percentage scores for
female candidates have fluctuated, but have generally improved over time.

personalized and focused approaches to teaching and learning.
