The standard deviation and CV showed some fluctuation but remained relatively
stable over the years. For soft furnishing and interior branding design, the mean
score remained relatively stable over the years, with a slight decrease from 84.3
in 2019 to 79.9 in 2022. The standard deviation and CV increased over the years,
indicating more variability in the scores among the students. For designing and
implementing furniture, the mean score remained relatively stable over the years,
with a slight increase from 79.7 in 2019 to 82.8 in 2022. The standard deviation and
CV showed some fluctuation but remained relatively stable over the years. For
practical IND, the mean score remained high and relatively stable over the years,
with a slight decrease from 94.8 in 2019 to 93.2 in 2022. The standard deviation
and CV increased over the years but remained low, indicating low variability in
the scores among the students. In summary, the table shows some variability in
the performance of students across different subjects in the field of designing and
performing interior design. Some subjects showed a decrease in mean scores
over the years, while others remained relatively stable. The standard deviation
and CV increased over the years for most subjects, indicating more variability in
the scores among the students.

Table 143 indicates the performance of students in different subjects over the
years using the mean score, standard deviation, and coefficient of variation (CV).

The mean score represents the average performance of students in a particular
subject, while the standard deviation shows the spread of the data around the
mean. The coefficient of variation (CV) is a measure of relative variability that is
useful for comparing the spread of data across different subjects, as it expresses
the standard deviation as a percentage of the mean.

264  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY

indicating increased relative variability.

In Tiles works, openings, and wall plastering, the percentage of female passes
remained consistent at around 97%, while the percentage of male passes
decreased slightly from 98.2% in 2019 to 96.4% in 2021, before increasing to
99.3% in 2022. Finally, for Practical MAS, both male and female passes remained
consistently high over the years, ranging from 99.0% to 99.8%.

In general, the performance of both males and females in the different subjects of
the masonry (MAS) program is relatively consistent, with some minor variations
in pass rates between the genders and across different years.

However, there has been a significant decline in the mean score for Tiles works,
openings, and wall plastering. The standard deviation and coefficient of variation
have increased for most subjects, indicating that the performance of the students
has become more varied over the years.

deviation (SD) has also increased over the years, indicating greater variability in
the scores. The coefficient of variation (CV) has decreased from 54.7 in 2019 to
22.3 in 2021 and further decreased to 34.0 in 2022.

Machine elements description: The mean score has remained relatively stable
over the years, with a slight increase from 42.3 in 2019 to 47.6 in 2021, followed by
a slight decrease to 48.7 in 2022. The SD has also fluctuated over the years, with
the highest value in 2019 and the lowest in 2021. The CV has decreased from 53.6
in 2019 to 40.9 in 2022.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  281
Machine tools operations: The mean score has increased from 56.7 in 2019 to 65.0
in 2021, followed by a slight decrease to 60.4 in 2022. The SD has also fluctuated
over the years, with the highest value in 2019 and the lowest in 2021. The CV has
decreased from 40.9 in 2019 to 26.9 in 2021, but increased to 41.7 in 2022.

Technical and assembly details drawing: The mean score has remained relatively
stable over the years, with a slight increase from 55.6 in 2019 to 56.7 in 2021,
followed by a slight decrease to 55.3 in 2022. The SD has also fluctuated over the
years, with the highest value in 2019 and the lowest in 2021. The CV has decreased
from 38.9 in 2019 to 25.8 in 2021, but increased to 39.6 in 2022. Welding technology:
The mean score has decreased from 74.0 in 2019 to 66.4 in 2021, followed by a
slight increase to 67.3 in 2022. The SD has also fluctuated over the years, with the
highest value in 2019 and the lowest in 2022. The CV has decreased from 27.2 in
2019 to 21.4 in 2021, but increased to 28.3 in 2022. Practical MPT: The mean score
has remained consistently high over the years, with a slight increase from 93.7
in 2019 to 93.6 in 2021, followed by a slight increase to 94.6 in 2022. The SD has
decreased over the years, indicating greater consistency in the scores. The CV
has also decreased over the years, indicating greater uniformity in the scores. In
summary, it is clear that while the performance of the student in hydraulic and
pneumatic systems has greatly improved over the years, performance in welding
technology has slightly decreased and then slightly increased. The other subjects
have shown relatively stable performance.

72.4% or higher`

higher, with PU and GA achieving a pass rate of 100% in all years. Overall, it seems
that schools owned by GA and PU have consistently achieved high pass rates
across all subjects and years, while schools owned by PR have shown more
variation in pass rates.

Table 181 presents the performance trends in different core subjects of RCT
(trade) over three years: 2019, 2021, and 2022. The percentage scores of each
subject in the three years are presented. The trends in the table show that overall,
the performance of students in RCT (trade) has improved significantly over the
three years, as most subjects achieved 100% in the last year. Bridge construction
technology and technical drawing had consistently high-performance levels of
99.5% in 2019 and remained high in 2021 and 2022. Construction materials testing
and Cost estimation and site management had lower scores in 2019, but both
showed significant improvements in 2021 and reached 100% in 2022. Similarly, Road
construction and maintenance had a slight decrease in 2021 but regained 100%
in 2022. Lastly, Practical RCT had a high-performance level of 99.5% in 2019 and
achieved a perfect score of 100% in 2021 and 2022. Overall, the table demonstrates
a positive trend in the performance of students in RCT (trade) across all subjects,
with most subjects achieving perfect scores in the last years.

Table 183, indicates the mean scores, standard deviations, and coefficient of
variation (CV) for subjects of Road construction (RCT) in the years 2019, 2021, and
2022. Looking at the data given, the following observations have been mentioned.
For Bridge Construction Technology: The mean score for Bridge Construction
Technology increased from 80.8 in 2019 to 84.6 in 2021, and further increased to
86.6 in 2022. The standard deviation also increased from 10.9 in 2019 to 9.7 in 2021,
and increased again to 11.2 in 2022. The coefficient of variation (CV) decreased
from 13.5 in 2019 to 9.4 in 2021, but increased to 11.2 in 2022. This indicates that
the performance of students in Bridge Construction Technology has generally
improved over time, but with a slightly higher degree of variability in the most
recent year. Construction Materials Testing: The mean score for Construction
Materials Testing increased significantly from 79.5 in 2019 to 88.9 in 2021, but
then dropped to 80.0 in 2022. The standard deviation also increased from 10.3 in
2019 to 8.8 in 2021, but increased again to 10.9 in 2022. The coefficient of variation
(CV) decreased from 12.9 in 2019 to 9.8 in 2021, but increased to 13.7 in 2022. This
indicates that while there was a significant improvement in student performance
in 2021, there was a decline in 2022, and the degree of variability in performance
increased.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  307
Cost Estimation and Site Management: The mean score for Cost Estimation and
Site Management increased significantly from 63.0 in 2019 to 80.5 in 2021, but
then dropped slightly to 78.6 in 2022. The standard deviation also decreased from
14.9 in 2019 to 11.3 in 2021, and decreased further to 11.9 in 2022. The coefficient
of variation (CV) decreased from 23.7 in 2019 to 14.0 in 2021, but increased to
15.1 in 2022. This indicates that there was a significant improvement in student
performance in 2021, but a slight decline in 2022, with a slightly higher degree of
variability in performance.

Road Construction and Maintenance: The mean score for Road Construction and
Maintenance increased significantly from 59.3 in 2019 to 83.8 in 2021, but then
dropped slightly to 80.9 in 2022. The standard deviation increased from 12.9 in
2019 to 10.0 in 2021, and increased further to 12.9 in 2022. The coefficient of variation
(CV) decreased from 21.8 in 2019 to 12.0 in 2021, but increased to 15.9 in 2022. This
indicates that while there was a significant improvement in student performance
in 2021, there was a decline in 2022, and the degree of variability in performance
increased.

Technical Drawing: The mean score increased from 77.1 in 2019 to 86.6 in 2021,
but then decreased to 80.4 in 2022. The standard deviation also increased from
11.9 in 2019 to 13.2 in 2022, while the CV increased from 15.5 in 2019 to 16.4 in 2022.
This suggests that there was greater variability in scores in 2022 compared to
2019. For Practical RCT, we can see that the mean score remained relatively
consistent across the years, with a high of 94.0 in 2021 and a low of 92.9 in 2022.
The standard deviation and CV remained relatively low across the years, with a
slight increase in 2022. This suggests that there was less variability in scores for
Practical RCT compared to others subjects. Overall, the data suggests that there
were fluctuations in scores and variability across the years for most of subjects,
while scores for Practical RCT remained consistent.

academic year. This could indicate a growing interest in the SCE trade or a higher
demand for skilled professionals in this field.

As indicated in table 186, the overall performance of students in the ceramic &

many of the students achieving perfect scores. In Ceramic Technology, History

could have affected the performance of the students.

Overall, these consistently high scores suggest that the students have a strong
grasp of the subject matter and have been well-prepared for the National
examinations. However, as with any exam results, it is important to consider other
factors that could influence performance, such as the quality of teaching and
resources available to the students.

Table 193 shows performance of students in solar energy trade in national
examinations across different subjects and years. In Basics of Electricity and
Electrical Measurement, there was a significant increase in the variation of scores
from 2019 to 2021, with the VC increasing from 9.75 to 41.31. However, in 2022, there
was a decrease in the VC to 26.3. This suggests that the performance of students
in this subject was more consistent in 2022 compared to 2021, but still more

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022        317
variable than in 2019. In Electrical Schematic Analysis, there was a substantial
increase in the variation of scores from 2019 to 2021, with the VC increasing
from 11.84 to 33.64. However, in 2022, there was a decrease in the VC to 16.0. This
suggests that the performance of students in this subject was more consistent in
2022 compared to 2021, but still more variable than in 2019. In Solar Power Plant
Design and Installation, there was also a significant increase in the variation of
scores from 2019 to 2021, with the VC increasing from 7.00 to 31.75. However, in
2022, there was a decrease in the VC to 12.6. This suggests that the performance
of students in this subject was more consistent in 2022 compared to 2021, but still
more variable than in 2019.

In Solar Water Heating Design and Installation, there was a substantial increase in
the variation of scores from 2019 to 2021, with the VC increasing from 7.58 to 26.40.
However, in 2022, there was a further decrease in the VC to 20.5. In Substation
Installation and Maintenance, there was a dramatic increase in the variation of
scores from 2019 to 2021, with the VC increasing from 4.95 to 45.93. However, in
2022, there was a decrease in the VC to 19.3. This suggests that the performance
of students in this subject was more consistent in 2022 compared to 2021, but
still more variable than in 2019. In Practical Solar Energy, there was a relatively
low variation of scores across all three years, with the VC ranging from 2.75 to
4.09. This suggests that the performance of students in this subject was more
consistent across the years. The variation in scores across the different subjects
and years suggests that the performance of students in some subjects was more
consistent than in others. The substantial increase in variation of scores in some
subjects from 2019 to 2021 is a cause for concern.

understanding of software development and are able to apply the knowledge
and skills learned effectively.

ownership. In 2019, the pass rate was 97.3% for GA, 92.9% for PR, and 92.9% for PU.
In 2021, the pass rate increased to 98.8% for GA, 93.8% for PR, and 95.8% for PU. In
2022, the pass rate remained high, with values of 99.3% for GA, 99.3% for PR, and
98% for PU. For the subject of "Embroidery material tools and equipment", we can
see that the pass rate is also consistently high across all three years and all three
types of school ownership. In 2019, the pass rate was 94.6% for GA, 87.3% for PR,
and 92.9% for PU. In 2021, the pass rate increased to 99.4% for GA, 97.5% for PR, and
97.4% for PU. In 2022, the pass rate remained high, with values of 100% for all three
types of school ownership.

For the subject of "Make fashion and embroidery design", it is also observed that
the pass rate is consistently high across all three years and all three types of
school ownership. In 2019, the pass rate was 97.3% for GA, 95.2% for PR, and 91.7%
for PU. In 2021, the pass rate slightly decreased to 96.3% for GA, 92.6% for PR, and
95.2% for PU. In 2022, the pass rate remained high, with values of 99.3% for GA,
100% for PR, and 100% for PU. For the subject of "Structure fabric", the pass rate is
consistently high across all three years and all three types of school ownership,
although there is a slight decrease in 2021 for PR and PU. In 2019, the pass rate was
94.6% for GA, 96.8% for PR, and 91.7% for PU. In 2021, the pass rate decreased to
89.0% for GA, 87.7% for PR, and 84.7% for PU. In 2022, the pass rate remained high,
with values of 98.6% for GA, 99.3% for PR, and 99.5% for PU.

For Structure Fabric, GA schools had the highest pass rate in 2019 and 2022, while
PR schools had the highest pass rate in 2021. PU schools had consistently high pass
rate across all three years, with a 99.5% pass rate in 2022. In Technical Drawing,
GA schools had the highest pass rate in all three years, with a pass rate of 99.3%
in 2021 and 2022. PR and PU schools also had consistently high pass rate across
all three years, with a perfect pass rate of 100% in 2021 and 2022. For the subject
of "Practical TAL", the pass rate is consistently high across all three years and all
three types of school ownership. In 2019, the pass rate was 97.3% for GA, 100% for
PR, and 98.8% for PU. In 2021, the pass rate remained high, with values of 100% for
all three types of school ownership. In 2022, the pass rate remained high, with
values of 100% for all three types of school ownership.

Overall, it appears that PU schools had consistently high pass rate in most subjects
across all three years.

the TV broadcasting station having a pass rate of 83.1% in 2021 and 96.6% in
2022. In conclusion, the table shows that practical TEL and RF and microwave
antenna system installation were the most common subjects performed in
the telecommunications trade during the given time frame. However, the pass
rate s of other subjects varied over the years, with some increasing and some
decreasing.

Table 208 displays the mean scores of different TEL (Telecommunication)
subjects in the years 2019, 2021, and 2022, along with their standard deviation (SD)
and coefficient of variation (CV) values. Looking at the mean scores, it reveals
that the performance of students in different TEL subjects has varied across the
years. Some subjects, such as Radio broadcasting station and RF and microwave
antenna system installation, have shown an improvement in mean scores over
the years. In contrast, subjects like GSM transmission system operations and TV
broadcasting station have shown a decline in mean scores over the years.

looking at the standard deviation (SD) values, which represent the amount of
variation or dispersion in the data. A higher SD indicates that the data points are
more spread out from the mean, whereas a lower SD indicates that the data points
are closer to the mean. We can see that the SD values for all the TEL subjects have
varied over the years. For example, the SD value for GSM transmission system
operations increased from 9.68 in 2019 to 14.3 in 2022, indicating that the scores
in this subject became more dispersed over time. Similarly, the SD value for TV
broadcasting station increased from 14.77 in 2019 to 20.2 in 2022, indicating a
higher level of variability in scores. Finally, let's consider the coefficient of variation
(CV) values, which represent the relative variability of the data compared to the
mean. A higher CV value indicates that the data has higher variability relative
to the mean, whereas a lower VC value indicates lower variability relative to the
mean. We can see that the CV values for all the TEL subjects have also varied over
the years. For example, the CV value for GSM transmission system operations
increased from 12.94 in 2019 to 36.6 in 2022, indicating a higher level of relative
variability in scores. Similarly, the CV value for RF and microwave antenna system
installation increased from 18.00 in 2019 to 23.2 in 2022. In summary, the analysis
of the table above shows that the performance of students in different TEL
subjects has varied over the years, and there has also been variation in the level
of dispersion and relative variability of scores across the years.

Broadcasting transmission system subject: The pass rates for the PR and PU
schools were high in 2019, with 83.1% and 89.3%, respectively. However, in 2021,
the pass rates dropped to 81.0% for PR schools and 100.0% for PU schools. In 2022,
the pass rates for PR schools increased slightly to 91.46%, while for PU schools, the
pass rates decreased to 96.23%. GSM transmission system operations subject:
The pass rates for both PR and PU schools were high in 2019, with 98.8% and 100.0%,
respectively. However, in 2021, the pass rates dropped significantly to 71.0% for PR
schools and remained the same for PU schools. In 2022, the pass rates for PR
schools improved slightly to 93.9%, while for PU schools, the pass rates decreased
slightly to 100.0%.

high in 2019, with 78.9% and 90.2%, respectively. In 2021, the pass rates improved

PR and PU schools further increased to 97.56% and 98.11%, respectively. RF and
microwave antenna system installation subject: The pass rates for both PR and
PU schools were high in 2019, with 97.0% and 98.2%, respectively. However, in 2021,
the pass rates dropped to 80.0% for PR schools and 94.9% for PU schools. In 2022,
the pass rates for both PR and PU schools slightly improved to 90.24% and 98.11%,
respectively. TV broadcasting station subject: The pass rates for both PR and PU
schools were high in 2019, with 71.7% and 87.5%, respectively. In 2021, the pass rates
improved to 76.0% for PR schools and 96.6% for PU schools. In 2022, the pass rates
for both PR and PU schools further increased to 100.0% and 100.0%, respectively.

Practical TEL: The pass rates for both PR and PU schools were high in all the
three years, with 98.8% and 100.0%, respectively, in 2019, and 100.0% and 100.0%,
respectively, in 2021 and 2022. In summary, the pass rates for TEL trade varied
across different subjects and years, with some subjects experiencing a drop-in
pass rates in 2021 but improving in 2022. Overall, the pass rates for PR and PU
schools were high for most subjects in all the three years.

of 90.1% in 2019, which increased to 100% in both 2021 and 2022. PR schools had
a pass rate of 41.2% in 2019, which increased to 89.0% in 2021 and then to 96.8%
in 2022. PU schools had a pass rate of 86.2% in 2019, 99.2% in 2021, and 83.1% in
2022. For Performing Community-Based Tourism subject: GA schools had a pass
rate of 100% in all three years.PR schools had a pass rate of 87.0% in 2019, which
decreased slightly to 82.4% in 2021 and then increased to 96.8% in 2022. PU schools
had a pass rate of 100% in 2019, 99.2% in 2021, and 94.4% in 2022. For Working as
a Guide subject: GA schools had a pass rate of 100% in all three years. PR schools
had a pass rate of 88.0% in 2019, which increased to 91.7% in 2021 and then to 99.4%
in 2022, and PU schools had a pass rate of 100% in all three years. In Practical TRS:
GA schools had a pass rate of 100% in all three years. PR schools had a pass rate
of 98.7% in 2019, which increased to 99.4% in 2021 and then to 100% in 2022, while
PU schools had a pass rate of 100% in all three years. In general, it seems that
all three school ownership categories (GA, PR, and PU) showed improvements in
pass rates over the years, especially in 2022. However, there were some subjects
and school ownership that had lower pass rates than others, such as Packaging
Tourism Products in PR and PU. It would be interesting to investigate the reasons
behind these differences and see how they can be improved.

Table 215 shows the total number of students who sat for the national exams in
the field of Welding (WEL) across the three academic years: 2019, 2021, and 2022.
The programs offered in the field of WEL include six subjects. Across the three
years, there is a clear increase in the number of students who attended each
subject. Specifically, the total number of students who sat for each subject in 2019
is significantly lower than the total number of students who sat in 2021 and 2022.
Each program had a total of 28 students in 2019, 89 students in 2021, and 134
students in 2022. This indicates a rise in the number of students that pursued WEL
programs across the three years, with an increase of 61 students (i.e., from 28 in
2019 to 89 in 2021) and an additional increase of 45 students (i.e., from 89 in 2021 to
134 in 2022). Overall, the table suggests that there is a growing interest in pursuing
programs related to Welding (WEL), and the number of students interested in the
field is increasing. This could be due to various factors, such as the growing demand
for skilled professionals in the field, the availability of diverse job opportunities,
and the rising importance of metalworking in different industries which is likely to
create more job opportunities and contribute to the overall economic growth. It is
noteworthy that all six subjects/programs had a similar increase in the number of
students attending across the three years, indicating that the interest in different
areas within the field of WEL is equally distributed.

Based on the data provided in table 2018, the performance of students in five
subjects, namely Advanced welding, Gas welding and brazing, Metal work,
Shielded metal arc welding (SMAW), and Technical drawing and DCG, over the
years 2019, 2021, and 2022 can be analysed as follows: Here is a breakdown of the
analysis by subject, using the mean score, standard deviation, and coefficient
of variation (CV): For Advanced welding: The mean score increased from 60.79
in 2019 to 76.1 in 2021 and then decreased to 73.4 in 2022. The standard deviation
increased from 10.47 in 2019 to 11.0 in 2021 and then further increased to 13.3 in

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022        343
2022. The coefficient of variation (CV) also increased from 17.22 in 2019 to 14.5
in 2021 and then further increased to 18.2 in 2022. This indicates an increase in
the variability of scores relative to the mean, which could imply a wider range
of performance among students. For Gas welding and brazing: The mean score
remained relatively stable over the three years, with a slight decrease from 77.57
in 2019 to 76.2 in 2022. The standard deviation fluctuated over the three years,
with a significant increase in 2022 compared to 2021. The coefficient of variation
(CV) also fluctuated over the years, with a slight increase in 2022 compared to
2021. This indicates some variability in performance among students but less so
than in Advanced welding. For Metal work: The mean score decreased from 75.21
in 2019 to 67.3 in 2021 but then increased to 70.3 in 2022. The standard deviation
increased over the years, with a significant jump from 13.5 in 2021 to 14.0 in 2022.
The coefficient of variation (CV) also increased over the years, with a significant
jump from 20.0 in 2021 to 19.9 in 2022. This indicates a wider range of performance
among students than in Gas welding and brazing. For Shielded metal arc welding
(SMAW): The mean score increased from 74.79 in 2019 to 82.9 in 2021 and then
further increased to 86.1 in 2022. The standard deviation decreased from 14.30 in
2019 to 10.9 in 2021 and then slightly increased to 10.4 in 2022. The coefficient of
variation (CV) also decreased from 19.13 in 2019 to 13.2 in 2021 and then slightly
increased to 12.0 in 2022. This indicates a more consistent range of performance
among students compared to Advanced welding and Metal work. For Technical
Drawing and DCG: The mean score of Technical Drawing and DCG increased
from 59.57 in 2019, to 66.5 in 2021, and slightly decreased to 66.0 in 2022. The
Standard deviation (SD) of Technical Drawing and DCG increased from 10.70 in
2019, to 16.1 in 2021, and decreased to 13.8 in 2022. The Coefficient of variation
(CV) of Technical Drawing and DCG increased from 17.97 in 2019, to 24.2 in 2021,
and decreased to 20.9 in 2022. Practical WEL: The mean score for Practical WEL
increased over the three years, from 89.64 in 2019 to 92.9 in 2021, and then to 93.9
in 2022. This indicates an overall improvement in the performance of students
in this subject. The standard deviation decreased over the three years, from
4.63 in 2019 to 4.1 in 2021, and then to 3.4 in 2022. This indicates that the scores
were less spread out and more tightly clustered around the mean in later years.
The coefficient of variation (CV), which is the ratio of the standard deviation to
the mean expressed as a percentage, decreased over the three years as well.
It was 5.16 in 2019, 4.5 in 2021, and 3.6% in 2022. This suggests that the relative
variability of the scores decreased over time. Overall, the data indicates that the
performance of students in WEL has improved over the three years, with scores
increasing, variability decreasing, and relative variability decreasing. However, it
is important to note that there is a need of further research to understand the
factors contributing to some differences.

significantly to 44.1% in 2022. On the other hand, Kinyarwanda has consistently
shown a high pass rate, with 97.38% of students passing in 2019, 91.68% in 2021,
and 96.98% in 2022. The performance in Mathematics I improved significantly
from 66.22% in 2019 to 85.19% in 2021, but then decreased slightly to 91.8% in 2022.
Mathematics II was not available in 2019 and 2021, but in 2022, 99.04% of students
passed the subject.

Français and Kiswahili have shown a significant increase in pass rates between
2019 and 2021. However, the pass rates for both subjects decreased in 2022, with
Français dropping from 68.59% to 57.69%, and Kiswahili dropping from 73.08% to
23.57%.

Overall, the data in the table suggests that there have been fluctuations in the
pass rates for different TVET subjects over the three-year period. Further analysis
would be needed to determine the reasons behind these fluctuations and to
identify any patterns or trends.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  353
Table 226 shows how male and female students performed on various TVET
disciplines in the national examination results. In English I, female studentspassed
at a higher rate (77.49%) than male (66.99%), students in 2019, but in the following
years, male students passed with a higher percentage (81.89% vs 88.9% in 2021
and 39.49% vs 48.3% in 2022). In Kinyarwanda, both male and female students
had high pass rates in all years, with female students having slightly higher pass
rates than male students (97.03% vs 97.69% in 2019, 90.05% vs 93.08% in 2021, and
96.17% vs 97.72% in 2022).

In Entrepreneurship, the female students had a higher pass rate than male students
in 2019 (79% vs 87%), but in the following years, male students had a higher pass
rate (80.82% vs 84.36% in 2021 and 69.63% vs 75.37% in 2022). In Mathematics I, the
female students had a lower pass rate than male students in 2019 (69% vs 65%),
but in the years that followed, female students had a higher pass rate than male
students (89.25% vs 83.53% in 2021 and 93.19% vs 91.32% in 2022). In Mathematics
II, there is no data for the pass rate of either male or female students. In Français,
female students had a lower pass rate than male students in 2019 (34% vs 51%),
but in the years that followed, female students had a higher pass rate than male
students (66.81% vs 73.02% in 2021 and 56.72% vs 60.08% in 2022). In Kiswahili,
female students had a higher pass rate than male students in 2019 (91% vs 92%),
but in the years that followed, female students had a lower pass rate than male
students (72.09% vs 75.54% in 2021 and 22.07% vs 23.57% in 2022).

Overall, we can observe that there are variations in the pass rates of male and
female students in different TVET subjects, and these variations change from
year to year. It is important to note that these numbers alone do not necessarily
indicate whether there are gender disparities in educational outcomes, as other
factors such as socioeconomic status, access to education, and cultural norms
could also play a role.

Table 227 provides the descriptive statistics for the variation coefficient (VC),
standard deviation (SD), and mean scores in different TVET subjects for three
consecutive years (2019, 2021, and 2022) in Rwanda's national examination
results. The variation coefficient is a measure of the dispersion of data, and a
lower coefficient indicates less variability in scores. The standard deviation
measures the spread of scores around the mean, and a lower standard deviation
indicates more consistent scores. In English I, the VC increased from 31.83% in 2019
to 41.97% in 2022, indicating an increase in variability among students' scores. In
Kinyarwanda, the VC decreased from 19.02% in 2019 to 18.74% in 2022, indicating
a more consistent performance among students. Entrepreneurship had the
highest VC in all three years, indicating a significant variability in scores among
students, with a decrease from 41.92% in 2019 to 37.35% in 2022. Mathematics I
had the highest VC in 2019 (64.57%) but showed a decrease to 43.32% in 2022,
indicating less variability in students' scores over the years. Mathematics II and
Kiswahili scores were not reported in 2019 and 2021. In 2022, Mathematics II had a
moderate VC (30.28%), while Kiswahili had a high VC (62.00%). In terms of mean
scores, Kinyarwanda consistently had the highest mean score in all three years,
while English I had the lowest. There was an increase in the mean scores for
Mathematics I and French from 2019 to 2022. Overall, this table provides insight
into the performance and variability of scores among students in different TVET
subjects in Rwanda's national examination results.

Table 228 shows the performance of students in various TVET subjects in the
national examination results, based on school ownership: Government Aided (GA),
Private (PR), and Public (PU). The table presents the frequency and percentage of
students who passed the exam in three consecutive years: 2019, 2021, and 2022.

In English I, the highest pass rate was observed in the GA schools in 2019 (88.45%),
which dropped to 60.98% in 2022. In contrast, the pass rate in PR schools increased
from 63.47% in 2019 to 80% in 2021, but then decreased to 37.01% in 2022. The pass
rate in PU schools was consistently high, ranging from 89.88% in 2019 to 93.92% in
2021, but dropped to 56.59% in 2022. In Kinyarwanda, the pass rate was consistently
high across all school ownership categories and years, ranging from 96.69% to
99.73% in GA schools, 89.36% to 97.28% in PR schools, and 98.7% to 99.59% in PU

356  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
schools. In Entrepreneurship, the pass rate was also consistently high in GA and
PU schools, ranging from 93.41% to 95.84% and 84.03% to 95.47%, respectively, but
lower in PR schools, ranging from 67.3% to 76.83%. In Mathematics I, the pass rate
increased in all school ownership categories and years, with the highest pass
rate observed in GA schools in 2022 (98.14%). The pass rate in PR schools also
increased, from 56.47% in 2019 to 89.57% in 2022, while the pass rate in PU schools
remained consistently high, ranging from 78.79% to 97.19%. In Mathematics II,
there is no data available for GA and PR schools, but the pass rate in PU schools
was consistently high, ranging from 99.13% to 99.33%. In French, the pass rate was
highest in GA schools in 2019 (93%) and then increased to 99.02% in 2021, but then
decreased to 91.1% in 2022. The pass rate in PR schools increased from 28% in 2019
to 61.09% in 2021, but then decreased to 56.27% in 2022. The pass rate in PU schools
was highest in 2022 (66.95%). In Kiswahili, the pass rate was highest in GA and PU
schools, ranging from 55.37% to 100% and 30.32% to 93.88%, respectively. However,
the pass rate in PR schools decreased from 91.75% in 2019 to 24.12% in 2022.

Overall, the table shows variations in the performance of TVET subjects across
different school ownership categories and years. It suggests that while some
schools consistently perform well in certain subject. According to chief marker's
report and the outcome of discussion with focused group of teachers, possible
reasons for candidates' low performance include language barrier, lack of
sufficient teaching and learning materials, family conflicts, Lack of prerequisite
in some subjects, Students' indiscipline, mismatch between taught content
and levels of candidates, lack of students' interest in some subjects like French,
confusing questions and instructions in the National examinations as well as
questions that require higher-order of thinking.

However, all these observations should be closely investigated to confirm whether
they constitute the real reason behind this performance trend.

REPORT ON ANALYSIS OF GENERAL EDUCATION, TTCs, AND TVET NATIONAL EXAMINATION RESULTS FOR 2019-2022  357
SECTION FOUR: CONCLUSION
AND RECOMMENDATION

This section provides conclusions and suggests recommendations based on the
findings of the analysis of the national examinations for the three school years
(2019, 2021 and 2022).

Conclusions

In conclusion, there was a progressive improvement in performance in national
examinations for primary, O-level, A-level, Teacher training colleagues as well as
TVET trades over the three years. In fact, in 2022, the general pass rates of students
in exams at all levels were significantly higher compared to 2019 and 2021. The
implication is that the new grading system had no impact on student failing or
passing. Despite the progressive improvement in general performance at different
levels, as earlier mentioned, in some subjects and trades, the performance was
poor and there were variations, especially pertaining to location (rural-urban),
ownership of school (public, district, private and government aid) and gender.
Based on the findings of the analysis, the following recommendations are made:

Recommendations

Based on the findings of the analysis, the following recommendations are made:

      The analysis of national examinations results for each learning cycle elicited
          pertinent information regarding pass rate and student performance in
          specific subjects. We recommend that NESA should have a long-term plan
          for adopting and implementing this practice every year.

      We recommend that in future, NESA should plan so that in future, end term
          III exam results are also analysed and comparison made with national
          exam results to provide a clear picture of student performance. It can also
          help teachers to evaluate the effectiveness of their teaching strategies.

      Despite the progressive improvement in general performance at different
          levels, as earlier mentioned, in some subjects and trades, the performance
          was poor and there were variations, especially pertaining to location (rural-
          urban), ownership of school (public, district, private and government aid)
          and gender. It is therefore, recommended that NESA, in collaboration with
          partners should conduct a comprehensive study to identify the factors that
          have contribute to disparities in performance by location, gender, district

358  NATIONAL EXAMINATION AND SCHOOL INSPECTION AUTHORITY
    and school ownership. The findings of the study could serve as the basis for
    development of evidence-based strategies that can enhance the quality of
    teaching and learning thereby addressing disparities between provinces,
    rural and urban schools. The study can also unveil best practices in high-
    performing districts and schools that can be adopted and implemented
    by those that perform poorly.

 Dissemination of the report of national examination analysis using different
    channels such as the NESA website and e-platform, media, and meetings
    with partners/stakeholders.
